MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
Weston S. Young, Chief Administrative Officer 
Candace Savage, Deputy Chief Administrative Officer 
FROM: 
Kelly Rados, Director of Recreation & Parks 
DATE: 
September 29, 2025 
SUBJECT: 
Worcester County Arts Council Mini Art Grant 
____________________________________________________________________________ 
The Recreation & Parks Department is requesting permission to apply for the Worcester County Arts Council Mini 
Grant in the amount of $500. The deadline to apply is October 15.  The grant requires a 1:1 match, which 
Recreation has budgeted in the Recreation Programs-Youth Recreational Programs account 100.1601.400.6175.090 
to meet this requirement. The grant funds will directly support artist compensation and art supplies needed for the 
proposed project.  
The proposed project includes creating a mosaic-style ceiling art project in the After School Zone room at the 
Recreation Center. This project will involve painting 10-20 ceiling tiles with a vibrant design that highlights 
recreation sports (volleyball, soccer, tennis, basketball, pickleball and indoor track) interwoven with words that 
celebrate teamwork, sportsmanship, and community.  
Local artist Olga Deshields will collaborate with our staff and participants, guiding the creative process and 
ensuring the project reflects both professional artistry and community engagement. Her expertise will help 
transform a functional ceiling into an inspiring work of art.  
Goals & Impact: 
•
Enhance the Environment: The painted ceiling tiles will brighten the ASZone, creating a colorful and
welcoming atmosphere for all who use the space.
•
Promote Positive Values: By incorporating words of sportsmanship and teamwork, the design will reinforce
the core values of recreation and youth development.
•
Foster Creativity: Involving participants in the process will encourage self-expression, appreciation for the
arts, and a sense of ownership in their community space.
•
Build Community Connections: Partnering with a local artist provides visibility for the arts and strengthens
ties between recreational programming and cultural enrichment.
Last year’s Worcester County Art Grant allowed our Department to work with local kids and families to paint a 
mural located at Showell Park. This sea turtle mural will complement the “under the sea” themed new playground 
and can be found near the concession stand. 
cc:  Ben Kirk, Recreation Superintendent 
      Kelly Buchanan, Program Manager IV

CAD Grant Guidelines Page 1 
 Revised: August 5, 2025 
GENERAL INFORMATION: 
The Community Arts Development (CAD) Grants program is administered by the Worcester County 
Arts Council, a not-for-profit organization. The funds distributed through this program are provided by 
the Maryland State Arts Council (MSAC) and, therefore, subject to availability, review, and audit by the 
MSAC. These grants are designed to strengthen local arts organizations and enhance cultural 
opportunities for Worcester County residents. 
ELIGIBILITY: 
To be eligible for this grant program, an organization must be: 
1. a not-for-profit, tax-exempt organization incorporated in Maryland, or a government-related
organization, such as a school or parks & recreation department, located in Worcester County.
2. Out of county sponsors will be considered if the project occurs in Worcester County.
Applications will NOT be accepted from: 
1. Individuals, commercial businesses, or for-profit ventures
2. Groups which discriminate based on age, race, sex, religion, sexual orientation or disability
3. Previous CAD grant recipients who have not properly submitted the required reports or
acknowledged WCAC and MSAC support for the grant.
MINI-GRANT REQUESTS: 
MINI-GRANT REQUESTS NOT TO EXCEED $500.00. 
The MINI GRANT program is intended for the following purposes: 
1. To provide funds to a new organization or new project that emerges between grant periods and
deserves immediate Arts Council support.
2. To provide funding for an established organization or project that encounters an urgent need or a
unique opportunity outside the regular grant submission deadlines of April 15th or October 15th.
COMMUNITY ARTS DEVELOPMENT (CAD) 
GRANT 
AND MINI-GRANT GUIDELINES 
ITEM 4
4-2

<!-- page 8 -->
CAD Grant Guidelines Page 2 
GRANT FUNDING LIMITS AND EQUITY PRIORITIES 
The Worcester County Arts Council (WCAC) is committed to ensuring equitable access to arts 
funding across our county and to supporting diverse and inclusive artistic programming. To 
promote responsible use of public funds and serve a broad range of community members, the 
following funding policies are in place: 
 ݤ
ݥ
ݦ
ݧ
ݨ Maximum Award Limit 
•
Organizations may request up to $5,000 per fiscal year in total grant funding from
WCAC.
•
This funding cap helps ensure equitable distribution of limited resources and allows a
broader range of groups to benefit from grant opportunities.
•
WCAC may offer partial funding if it allows the Council to support more projects
countywide.
 ڙ
ښ
ڛ Countywide Distribution 
•
WCAC aims to support projects throughout all areas of Worcester County.
•
Applications are encouraged from rural, small-town, and geographically
underrepresented communities to ensure a balanced reach across the region.
 ᇤ
ᇥ
ᇦ
ᇧ
ᇨ Prioritizing Underserved Populations 
WCAC prioritizes funding for projects that: 
•
Serve historically under-served or marginalized populations, including individuals with
disabilities, those who are institutionalized, older adults, and low-income communities.
•
Increase access to the arts for individuals or communities with limited exposure to
cultural opportunities.
•
Celebrate and preserve Worcester County’s rich cultural heritage by supporting diverse
artistic expressions from all ethnic and racial backgrounds.
•
Recognize the essential role of the arts in every community, with a focus on supporting
high-quality programs that reach small towns and rural areas throughout the county.
 ഹ
ഺ഻഼
ഽ
ാ Evaluation Criteria 
Applications will be reviewed based on the following key factors: 
•
Artistic merit and community impact
•
Alignment with WCAC’s mission and commitment to art inclusivity
•
Geographic and demographic reach
•
Fiscal responsibility and clarity of budget
•
Past funding history (with new applicants and new projects benefiting under-served
groups given strong consideration)
ITEM 4
4-3

<!-- page 9 -->
CAD Grant Guidelines Page 3 
REQUIREMENTS FOR WORCESTER COUNTY ARTS COUNCIL GRANTS 
& MINI-GRANTS 
1.
Proposed project must be sponsored or presented by a not-for-profit, tax-exempt organization
incorporated in Maryland, or a government-related organization, such as a school or parks &
recreation department, located in Worcester County. Out of county sponsors will be considered if
the project occurs in Worcester County.
2. 
Projects already completed will not be considered. 
3.
Project must be open to the general public, beyond the organization, without discrimination
based on race, color, national origin, sex, or age.
4.
Site of project must be accessible to persons with disabilities.
5.
Grants must be matched one-for-one by your organization funds. Earned income from the
project and other grant money may be used. Donated goods may not constitute part of the
match. Grant funds awarded may be less than your request. In the event of cancellation,
grant funds received must be returned.
6.
The grantee must notify the Worcester County Arts Council in writing of any major changes to the
project as described in the original grant application. If changes occur, the grantee will be required
to submit a Grant Amendment for review and approval. If the project is rescheduled to a date
outside of the approved grant period, the grant will be canceled, a new application must be
submitted, and any grant funds received must be returned.
7.
Grantee must supply Worcester County Arts Council with six (6) complimentary tickets to the funded
event/project (if applicable).
8.
Credit for the grant must be given to the Worcester County Arts Council and the Maryland State Arts
Council  in all brochures, programs, public service announcements, press releases, social media posts,
and advertising.
a)
Logo of the Worcester County Arts Council must be included in all promotional materials.
b)
Failure to acknowledge the Councils as a funding source will disqualify future requests.
c) 
The Worcester County Arts Council poster or banner must be displayed at the project site.
The poster is available for pick up at the WCAC office.
9.
A final report must be completed within 30 days after the completion of the project and submitted
via email to: anna@worcestercountyartscouncil.org. If the project takes place in June, the report
is due no later than June 30. Future grant requests will not be considered if final report is not
received on time, as required.
The final report must include: 
a)
An image (jpg format) from the event/project
b)
A copy of one of the following: flyer, program, press announcement, advertising, etc.
acknowledging WCAC grant support
c)
A copy of the grantee’s letters sent to local officials (list enclosed in policies letter)
10. Grantee should attend the Worcester County Arts Council’s Annual Arts Celebration, which is
held in the final quarter of each fiscal year.
11. Grantee is strongly encouraged to become a member of the Worcester County Arts Council.
Membership application is available at www.worcestercountyartscouncil.org or by calling
410- 641-0809.

4-4

<!-- page 10 -->
CAD Grant Guidelines Page 4 
GRANT RESTRICTIONS AND ELIGIBLE USE OF FUNDS 
To ensure the proper stewardship of public funds and alignment with the mission of the Arts Council, 
all grant recipients must adhere to the following use-of-funds guidelines: 
Ineligible Use of Grant Funds 
Grant funds may not be used for the following purposes: 
1.
Sub-granting or re-granting to other organizations or individuals.
2.
Awards, prizes, or cash recognition distributed through contests, competitions, juried shows, or
raffles.
3.
Honorariums or stipends for individuals serving solely as judges for art competitions.
4.
Projects primarily intended for fundraising for the sponsoring organization, beyond the cost of
the event itself. Grant funds must be applied strictly to the artistic component of the project.
5.
Capital improvements, including construction, renovations, or installation of permanent fixtures.
6.
Purchase of permanent equipment, furniture, or technology not directly related to the delivery
of the artistic program.
7.
General operating expenses such as administrative salaries, office rent, utilities, or insurance.
8.
Social functions, including parties, receptions, or other hospitality-related events.
9.
Travel or lodging expenses, including hotel accommodations.
10. Uniforms, costumes for staff, or non-artistic apparel.
11. Projects promoting political or religious viewpoints, or any activity that seeks to proselytize
political or religious beliefs or actions.
Eligible Use of Grant Funds 
Grant funds must support the artistic and cultural value of the project and should demonstrate a clear 
benefit to the community.  
Eligible uses include, but are not limited to: 
1.
Artistic Fees for artists, musicians, dancers, actors, or cultural professionals directly involved in
the public presentation or instruction of art.
2.
Production Costs such as lighting, sound, costumes (for performances), set construction, or
technical staff.
3.
Art Supplies and Materials for workshops, community projects, or hands-on activities.
4.
Marketing and Promotion of the event or program (e.g., posters, flyers, website banners).
5.
Venue Rental Fees when the project requires use of a space for an exhibit, performance, or
workshop.
6.
Teaching Artist Compensation for classes, lectures, or demonstrations open to the public or
specific community groups.
All expenditures must occur within the approved grant period and must be directly tied to the 
approved project scope. 
APPLICATION INSTRUCTIONS:

4-5

CAD Grant Guidelines Page 5 
1.
Applications must strictly adhere to deadlines.
2.
All portions of the form should be completed and all figures clearly totaled.
3. 
 Grant application with all required attachments must be combined into one pdf document and    
         submitted via email  to: curator@worcestercountyartscouncil.org   Please include CAD GRANT

4.
Grantee is requested to add Worcester County Arts Council to its mailing lists.
APPLICATION PROCEDURE AND DEADLINES: 
Grant Periods: 
1.
Grants may be made for projects occurring during the 6-month periods of July through December
(deadline to submit April 15th) and January through June (deadline to submit October 15th).
2.
Organizations that hold projects year-round or in both 6-month periods may request grants that
cover the entire fiscal year of July through the following June. Annual grant applications must be
submitted by April 15th.
Grant Request Review Procedure: 
1.
Grant requests will be reviewed by the Executive Director and then submitted to Grants Committee
for review.
2.
Grants Committee presents awards recommendations to the Board of Directors, which makes
the final funding decision.
3.
Applicant will receive a notification letter stating the Board of Directors decision approximately
one month after the application submission deadline.
 Grant Payment Schedule: 
1.
If awarded a grant, the grantee will be required to sign a Grant Policies form, which serves as a
formal grant agreement and acknowledgement of the grant.
2.
Following the signing of the Grant Policies form, a check for 75% of the grant award will be
issued a week prior to the event/project.
3.
The remaining payment will be released upon successful submission of the final grant report,
which is due within 30 days after completion of the event/project.
GRANT PAYMENT SCHEDULE AND GRANT AMOUNTS ARE SUBJECT TO RECEIPT OF FUNDS 
FROM THE MARYLAND STATE ARTS COUNCIL. 
Do not make expenditures in advance of the grant award notification that you cannot cover with 
organizational funds. 
QUESTIONS and TECHNICAL ASSISTANCE:

SUBJECT: 
Mystic Harbor – Replacement Control Panel for Station F 
Public Works is requesting Commissioner approval to award the replacement control panel 
and enclosure for Mystic Harbour Pump Station F to Lywood Automation, LLC. in the amount of 
$130,555.00. Funds are available in the Mystic Harbour Capital Equipment account number 
545.9010.080. This item was included in the approved FY 26 budget in the amount of $200,000.  
The request to directly award to Lywood Automation is a potential cost saving measure. 
Typically, work over $25,000 is bid out, however to bid the work out, engineered electrical 
drawings and specifications would need to be generated at an estimated additional cost $30,000-
$50,000. The electrical work is relatively straight forward, by bringing in several contractors to 
view the equipment needing to be installed, the contractors understood what was necessary without 
the need for drawings and specifications. Public Works reached out to other local contractors with 
experience working on pump station control panels and only Lywood responded with a not-to-
exceed maximum price.  
The replacement of this control panel is important to notify staff of any irregular operations 
while away from the lift station.  It communicates with the operators who are on call for any 
alarms.  Without those alarms the station could have inoperable pumps, potentially causing a 
danger to the environment, and lack of service to the surrounding residents/customers.  The 
existing facility utilizes a system installed originally in 1997 when the facility was built.   
Please let me know if there are any questions. 
Attachment 
CC: 
Quinn Dittrich 
Chris Clasing 
Tony Fascelli

SUBJECT: 
FY 26 Slurry Seal Contract 
Public Works is requesting Commissioner approval to piggyback off Cecil County’s 
contract with Slurry Pavers for the application of slurry seal on county roads at a price of 
$3.93/square yard. Funding in the amount of $500,000.00 for the purchase of slurry seal was 
approved in the current FY26 operating budget in the general fund account 100.1202.6140.010. 
In 2023, the Commissioners approved piggybacking on Howard County’s contract, but that 
contract expired this past July. Cecil County competitively bid these services for a one-year term 
with two one-year renewal options.  Their total estimated contract over those 3 years is $2,464,518.  
By utilizing this existing contract, the county will take advantage of Cecil County’s increased 
buying power, and we anticipate work starting next spring.  The planned list of roads to be slurry 
sealed is attached. 
Please let me know if there are any questions. 
Attachment 
CC: 
Kevin Lynch

SUBJECT: 
Proposal for Engineering Services – Hotel Road Bridge Replacement 
Public Works is requesting Commissioner approval to contract with Davis, Bowen, and 
Friedel, Inc. for engineering services for the Hotel Road Bridge replacement project.  The total cost 
of engineering services for this bridge would be $24,100.00 (see attached document). Funding is 
available in Road Maintenance State Aid Road Construction account 100.1202.6600.030.    
Davis, Bowen, and Friedel, Inc. have provided engineering and design services for the 
County since the mid 80’s and have always provided excellent service. Their knowledge of the area 
as well as State Aid requirements is an advantage which will ensure we are getting a quality service. 
This bridge has a bridge sufficiency rating below 50 and was recommended by the consultant for 
bridge replacement during our bridge inspection cycle.   
Please let me know if there are any questions. 
Attachments 
cc: 
Kevin Lynch, Roads Superintendent

datum will be established at the site.  We will locate the bridge, abutments, guardrails, utility poles, 
marked utilities, channel, embankments, relevant topography, pertinent trees and wetlands. 
STATE HIGHWAY ADMINISTRATION:  
$1,600. 
Complete the State Highway Administration “Environmental Assessment Form”.   Submit letters of 
intent to state agencies including, DNR Wildlife & Heritage Division, DNR Environmental Review 
Unit, Maryland Historic Trust and U.S. Fish & Wildlife Service.  Coordinate project with Worcester 
County/SHA Federal Aid Liaison. 
CONSTRUCTION DOCUMENTS:   
          $11,600. 
Prepare plans and specifications adequate for bidding and construction containing the 
following: 
1.
Site Plan drawing showing existing and new bridge location, approach roadway paving,
approach vehicular railing, grading, utilities and erosion/sediment control structures.
2.
Drawings will include pertinent existing conditions and demolition information.
3.
Drawings will include details for new laminated timber superstructure, glue-laminated timber
pile caps, driven timber pile foundation, timber or vinyl sheetpile abutments, timber bridge
railing and W-Beam approach vehicular railing.
4.
Pertinent technical specifications will be included on the plans providing load requirements for
the bridge supplier, material and treatment specifications, demolition requirements, shop
drawing requirements and factors pertinent to construction.

GENERAL TERMS AND CONDITIONS 
1.
These General Terms and Conditions are an integral part of the Proposal dated July 16, 2025
(“Proposal”) between Davis, Bowen & Friedel, Inc. (“DBF”) and Worcester County. (“CLIENT”)
(collectively, the “Parties”; individually, a “Party”).
2.
The Proposal shall be valid for sixty (60) calendar days from its date.  Upon execution by CLIENT,
the Proposal, including these General Terms and Conditions, shall comprise the Parties’ Agreement
for DBF’s services (collectively, the “Agreement”).

a.
The CLIENT warrants that DBF will be able to proceed with the project outlined in the
Proposal (the “Project”) without reasonable or agreed upon delays or interruption.
b.

Agreement, the method of compensation to accomplish the additional services shall either
be agreed to in writing prior to DBF undertaking the additional services or, otherwise, DBF
shall be compensated at its then prevailing schedule of rates (“DBF’s Rate Schedule”).
c.

unanticipated factors or circumstances often arise that can change the direction of the
Project and the scope or nature of DBF’s anticipated services.  DBF will inform the
CLIENT promptly if it becomes aware of such factors or circumstances.  Upon receipt of
such notice, CLIENT will negotiate in good faith for a change in Project scope and DBF’s
compensation.
d.
If any mark-outs or staking are destroyed by an act of nature or any person other than DBF,
they shall be re-marked or re-staked as an extra cost and compensated in the manner
provided in the Proposal or, if not specified, according to DBF’s Rate Schedule.
e.
After final acceptance of any of DBF’s plans by the CLIENT and /or the appropriate public
agencies, DBF shall not be required to make additional changes thereto unless the method
of compensation to accomplish said change(s) shall be agreed to in writing by CLIENT or,
in the absence of express agreement, DBF shall be compensated for such additional work
according to DBF’s Rate Schedule.
f.

information known to CLIENT with respect to the existence or suspected existence
at, on, or under the Project site (the “Site”) of any hazardous substances and/or
asbestos as such hazardous substances are defined in the Federal Comprehensive
Environment Response, Compensation and Liability Act of 1980, the Resource
Conservation and Recovery Act of 1976 or under any laws of similar import now
or hereafter existing.

Re: 
Request to Schedule Public Hearing: Worcester County Housing Study 
I am requesting that the Worcester County Commissioners schedule a required public hearing for 
Tuesday, November 4, 2025, associated with the Housing Study which was funded under the Maryland 
Community Development Block Grant Program. A draft public hearing notice is attached. 
Representatives from Matrix Design Group will attend the public hearing to present their findings and 
recommendations. A copy of the report can be found on the county’s website: 
https://www.co.worcester.md.us/departments/drp/2025-Housing-Study 
The Housing Study addresses the following topics: 
•
An update to the 2004 Substandard Housing Survey to identify and map substandard single-
family housing units as indicated by the condition of the exterior structure and grounds.
•
An update to the Affordable Housing report from 2004.
•
An analysis of current demographic, economic and housing market conditions.
•
A household and housing demand forecast for the County to the year 2050.
•
Identification of current programs and potential funding sources that could be utilized to
improve the County’s housing stock.
•
Provide recommendations that will allow the County to develop an overall housing program
that will improve the existing housing stock, target areas identified in the study to provide the
highest and best use of funding and provide a greater supply of affordable housing.
Thank you for your attention to this matter. Should you have any questions or require additional 
information, please do not hesitate to contact me.

Subject: 
   Atlantic Coastal Bays Critical Area Amendment/Refinement Request 
 
Date: 
 September 26, 2025 
 
 
 
 
Natural Resources has received an amendment/refinement request from Mr. Mark Cropper 
of the Law Offices of Ayres, Jenkins, Gordy & Almand, P.A., to seek a mapping mistake on the 
lands of Buck Browns, LLC located at Tax Map 25 Parcel 298, also known as 10702 Assateague 
Rd.  The applicant would like to reclassify 2.19 acres, as shown on Attachment 3, from Resource 
Conservation Area (“RCA”) to Limited Development Area (“LDA”).   
   
Following receipt of this request, staff conducted a thorough review including preliminary 
comments from the State Critical Area Commission (Attachment 2). If the request is approved by 
the County Commissioners following a public hearing in accordance with NR 3-110(3)(D), the 
request is then forwarded to the Critical Area Commission for approval or denial. 
 
In accordance with § NR 3-110, Official Critical Area Map amendments can only be 
granted by the County Commissioners upon proof of a mistake in the existing land use area. Per 
Maryland Natural Resource Article § 8-1809.(j)(2) to approve a mapping mistake it must meet the 
following requirements: 
 
(i) 
Conforms to the State critical area mapping criteria. 
 
ITEM 11
11 - 2

<!-- page 48 -->
Citizens and Government Working Together 
WORCESTER COUNTY GOVERNMENT CENTER        1 WEST MARKET STREET, ROOM 1306        SNOW HILL, MARYLAND 21863 
TEL: 410-632-1220     FAX: 410-632-2012 
As you are aware, the RCA designation within the Critical Area program is the most 
restrictive and is characterized by wetlands, forests, agricultural lands and various other nature 
dominated environments. Development, redevelopment, and land use activities occurring within 
this designation shall take place in a manner to conserve, protect, and enhance ecological values 
of the Critical Area as well as maintain and support agriculture, forestry, aquaculture, and fishery 
activities. Commercial activities are not permitted in the RCA and are considered nonconforming 
uses. 
Per § NR 3-107(a), LDAs are those areas that are currently developed in low- or moderate-
intensity uses. They also contain areas of natural plant and animal habitats. The quality of run-off 
from these areas has not been substantially altered or impaired. At the time of the initial mapping, 
these areas shall have at least one of the following features: 
(1)
Housing density ranging from one dwelling unit per five acres up to four dwelling
units per acre;
(2)
Areas not dominated by agricultural, wetland, forest, barren land, surface water,
or open space;
(3)
Areas meeting the conditions of an Intensely Developed Area but comprising less
than twenty acres; or
(4)
Areas having public sewer or public water, or both.
The request meets this requirement, specifically number one and two as zoning at the time 
of the 2002 Atlantic Coastal Bays Critical Area map adoption was commercial. It is improved with 
a single-family dwelling and a building that has been used for various commercial activities. 
(ii)
For areas included in the critical area due to remapping, is based on land uses
or natural features in existence at the time of the remapping. 
A rezoning application was approved by the County Commissioners on July 23, 1985, to 
change the district from A-1 Agricultural to B-1 Neighborhood Business. A business/commercial 
type of zoning has continued, with the property today zoned C-1 Commercial.  
(iii)
Follows the local jurisdiction’s documented mapping methodology for critical
area classifications at the time of original program adoption. 
Worcester County Critical Area Law, § NR 3-107(a) mimics the State’s mapping criteria 
for LDAs as listed above in (i).

11 - 3

WORCESTER COUNTY GOVERNMENT CENTER        1 WEST MARKET STREET, ROOM 1306        SNOW HILL, MARYLAND 21863 
TEL: 410-632-1220     FAX: 410-632-2012 
At the time of map adoption, most commercially zoned areas throughout the County were 
designated as LDA.  
(iv)
Is consistent with the purposes, policies, and goals of this subtitle and all
criteria of the Commission. 
The proposed remapping is consistent with the Atlantic Coastal Bays Critical Area program 
as this commercially zoned property is more appropriately designated as LDA instead of RCA. 
The Critical Area Commission provided preliminary comments in a letter from Kathryn 
Hayden, dated May 29, 2025, (Attachment 2). The Commission requested additional information 
that has been addressed by County staff above and the attachments.  
Natural Resources staff recommends approval of the request due to a mistake at the time 
the original maps were adopted as addressed in the criteria listed above. The mapping process did 
not take into consideration the business/commercial zoning at the time of map adoption. The RCA 
designation is a limiting factor in a potential commercial redevelopment of the property. 
As noted above, this request is to introduce to the County Commissioners for their 
consideration at a public hearing for the proposed amendment/refinement to reclassify 2.19 acres 
of land, designated as RCA, to LDA, located on the lands of Buck Browns LLC. located at Tax 
Map 25 Parcel 298, also known as 10702 Assateague Road.

Re: 
10702 Assateague Rd – Mapping Mistake Amendment 
Dear Mr. Soper, 
Thank you for submitting information regarding the above-referenced amendment to Worcester 
County’s Critical Area Map to our office for preliminary comment. The County proposes to 
change the critical area designation at 10702 Assateague Road, Berlin MD (Tax Map 25, Parcel 
298) due to a mapping mistake. The property is currently designated Resource Conservation
Area (RCA). The County requests that the designation be changed to Limited Development Area
(LDA). There are two existing buildings onsite, a house and a commercial/mixed use building
that was previously used as a restaurant. The site is located within the Coastal Bays portion of
Worcester County’s Critical Area.
Site History and Background 
The property is 2.18 acres in size and prior to 1985, it was zoned Agricultural District (A-1). In 
1985, the property was rezoned to Neighborhood Business District (B-1) due to its use as a 
country store. The property was zoned B-1 when the Coastal Bays were added to the Worcester 
County Critical Area Program in 2002. The purpose of the B-1 zoning district is to provide for 
small neighborhood commercial areas to serve the convenience shopping and service needs of 
the local community. In 1994, the country store on the property changed to a restaurant. In the B-
1 zoning district, restaurants, retail business, and general offices are an allowed use by right.  
In 2009, the property was rezoned C-1. The B-1 district no longer exists within the County’s 
code. The purpose and intent of the C-1 district is to “provide for convenient commercial areas 
strategically based to serve the day-to-day shopping and service needs of the local 
neighborhood” and is specific to “small-scale commercial operations”. Uses that are allowed 
within the C-1 district include retail businesses and general offices. Uses that may be permitted 
by special exception include neighborhood restaurants. From preliminary plans submitted 
separately from this map amendment application, it is our understanding that the property owner 
wishes to maintain the house on the property and, expand the existing commercial/mixed use 
building onsite for retail and office space. 
Attachment 2
ITEM 11
11 - 33

<!-- page 79 -->
Preliminary Comments 
Natural Resources Article 8-1809 (j)(2) states that a change to a Critical Area designation may 
be granted by a local approving authority if the proposed Critical Area classification: 
1. Conforms to the State Critical Area mapping criteria;
2. (a) Is based on land uses or natural features in existence as of June 1, 2002 in the
Atlantic Coastal Bays; or
2. (b) For areas included in the Critical Area due to remapping, is based on land uses or
natural features in existence at the time of the remapping.
3. Follows the local jurisdiction’s documented mapping methodology for Critical Area
classifications at the time of original program adoption; and
4. Is consistent with the purposes, policies, and goals of this subtitle and all criterial of
the Commission.
COMAR 27.01.02.04.A. lists the State Critical Area mapping criteria for LDAs: 
Limited Development Areas are those areas which are currently developed in low or moderate 
intensity uses. They also contain areas of natural plant and animal habitats, and the quality of 
runoff from these areas has not been substantially altered or impaired. These areas shall have at 
least one of the following features: 
1. Housing density ranging from one dwelling unit per 5 acres up to four dwelling units
per acre;
2. Areas not dominated by agriculture, wetland, forest, barren land, surface water, or
open space;
3. Areas meeting the conditions of Regulation .03A1 but no .03B2, of this regulation;
4. Areas having public sewer or public water, or both.
The County will need to provide additional information detailing how the proposed mapping 
mistake meets the above requirements laid out in the law and the mapping criteria laid out in the 
regulations.  
Thank you for the opportunity to provide preliminary review and comment. Please contact me 
with any questions: 410.260.3479 or kathryn.hayden@maryland.gov.  
Sincerely, 
1 COMAR 27.01.02.03A: Intensely Developed Areas are those areas where residential, commercial, institutional 
and/or industrial, developed land uses predominate, and where relatively little natural habitat occurs. These areas 
shall have at least one of the following features: 
1.
Housing density equal to or greater than four dwelling units per acre;
2.
Industrial, institutional, or commercial uses are concentrated in the area; or
3.
Public sewer and water collection and distribution systems are currently serving the area and housing
density is greater than three dwelling units per acre;
2 COMAR 27.01.02.03.B: (1) Except as authorized under B(2) of this regulation, the features in A(1)-(3) of this 
regulation shall be located in an area of at least 20 adjacent acre, or that entire upland portion of the Critical Area 
within the boundary of a municipality, whichever is less; (2) The features may be located in an area of less than 20 
adjacent acres if (a) as part of a local program, the Commission has approved an alternative standard for designation 
of an IDA; and (b) the area is part of a growth allocation approved by the Commission.

   Atlantic Coastal Bays Critical Area Amendment/Refinement Request 
 
Date: 
 September 26, 2025 
 
 
 
 
Natural Resources has received an amendment/refinement request from Mr. Mark Cropper 
of the Law Offices of Ayres, Jenkins, Gordy & Almand, P.A., to seek a mapping mistake on the 
lands of Olivia H Phillips & Olivia Harrison Phillips Trustee located at Tax Map 22 Parcel 268 
bordering on Center Drive and North Road.  The applicant would like to reclassify approximately 
6.60 acres that comprise Lots 41-59 from the plat created July 24, 1950 and duly recorded in the 
Worcester County Land Records, from Resource Conservation Area (“RCA”) to Intensely 
Development Area (“IDA”).   
   
Following receipt of this request, staff conducted a thorough review including preliminary 
comments from the State Critical Area Commission (Attachment 2). If the request is approved by 
the County Commissioners following a public hearing in accordance with NR 3-110(3)(D), the 
request is then forwarded to the Critical Area Commission for approval or denial. 
 
In accordance with § NR 3-110, Official Critical Area Map amendments can only be 
granted by the County Commissioners upon proof of a mistake in the existing land use area. Per 
Maryland Natural Resource Article § 8-1809.(j)(2) to approve a mapping mistake it must meet the 
following requirements: 
 
(i) 
Conforms to the State critical area mapping criteria. 
ITEM 12
12 - 2

<!-- page 84 -->
Citizens and Government Working Together 
 
WORCESTER COUNTY GOVERNMENT CENTER        1 WEST MARKET STREET, ROOM 1306        SNOW HILL, MARYLAND 21863 
TEL: 410-632-1220     FAX: 410-632-2012 
 
When initially mapped, this property was designated as RCA due to the conditions and 
environmental aspects present at the time of mapping. As you are aware, the RCA designation 
within the Critical Area program is the most restrictive and is characterized by wetlands, forests, 
agricultural lands and various other nature dominated environments. Development, 
redevelopment, and land use activities occurring within this designation shall take place in a 
manner to conserve, protect, and enhance ecological values of the Critical Area as well as maintain 
and support agriculture, forestry, aquaculture, and fishery activities.   
 
Per § NR 3-106(a), IDAs consistent of residential, commercial, institutional, and/or 
industrial uses predominate and where relatively little natural habitat occurs or remains. At the 
time of the initial mapping, these areas shall have at least one of the following features: 
 
 
 
1) Housing density equal to or greater than four dwelling units per acre; 
 
 
 
2) Industrial, institutional, or commercial uses are concentrated in the area; or 
 
(3) Public sewer and water collection and distribution systems are currently serving the 
area, and housing density is greater than three dwelling units per acre. 
 
(4) In addition, these features shall be concentrated in an area of at least twenty adjacent 
acres or that entire upland portion of the Critical Area within the boundary of a 
municipality, whichever is less, unless: 
 
A. The Commission has approved an alternative standard for designation of an 
Intensely Developed Area; and 
 
B. The area is part of a growth allocation approved by the Commission. 
 
 
The request meets this requirement, specifically number three as the existing lots would be 
served by existing public utilities and the surrounding lots of similar size are mapped IDA. 
 
(ii) 
For areas included in the critical area due to remapping, is based on land uses 
or natural features in existence at the time of the remapping. 
 
The lots were platted in 1950 and are legally existing. During the original mapping the tax 
maps used did not reflect the platted lots. As other lots of similar sizes in the area were mapped 
IDA, these lots would have been mapped IDA as well.

12 - 3

<!-- page 85 -->
Citizens and Government Working Together 
 
WORCESTER COUNTY GOVERNMENT CENTER        1 WEST MARKET STREET, ROOM 1306        SNOW HILL, MARYLAND 21863 
TEL: 410-632-1220     FAX: 410-632-2012 
(iii)    Follows the local jurisdiction’s documented mapping methodology for critical 
area classifications at the time of original program adoption. 
 
Worcester County Critical Area Law, § NR 3-106(a) mimics the State’s mapping criteria 
for IDAs as listed above in (i). 
 
As discussed in (ii) above, other lots of similar size in this area of the Atlantic Coastal Bays 
Critical Area are designated as IDA. 
 
(iv) 
Is consistent with the purposes, policies, and goals of this subtitle and all 
criteria of the Commission. 
 
The proposed remapping is consistent with the Atlantic Coastal Bays Critical Area program 
as other lots of similar sizes in this area were mapped IDA and these lots would be served by 
existing public utilities.   
 
 
 
The Critical Area Commission provided preliminary comments in a letter from Kathryn 
Hayden, dated June 25, 2025, (Attachment 2). The Commission requested additional information 
that has been addressed by County staff above and the attachments.  
  
 
      
Natural Resources staff recommends approval of the request due to a mistake at the time 
the original maps were adopted as addressed in the criteria listed above. The mapping process did 
not take into consideration the existing lots 41-59 as these were not shown on the tax maps used 
to prepare the Atlantic Coastal Bays Critical Area maps.  
 
As noted above, this request is to introduce to the County Commissioners for their 
consideration of a public hearing for the proposed amendment/refinement to reclassify 
approximately 6.60 acres of land as shown on the recorded plat dated July 24, 1950, designated as 
RCA, to IDA, located on the lands of Olivia H Phillips & Olivia Harrison Phillips Trustee located 
at Tax Map 22 Parcel 268 Lots 41-59.

designation be changed to Intensely Developed Area (IDA). There is currently one house on the 
property. The site is located within the Atlantic Coastal Bays portion of Worcester County’s 
Critical Area. 
 
The property is 34.4 acres in size. A plat dated July 24, 1950 shows a subdivision on the property 
with 19 lots (lots 41 through 59) designated along Center Drive and North Road and surrounding 
one of the wetlands on the property. The properties immediately on the other side of Center 
Drive and North Road are designated IDA. From information submitted with this application, we 
understand that lots 41 through 59 have an equivalent dwelling unit (EDU) allocation for sewer. 
The underlying zoning district for this property and the surrounding neighborhood is R-2 
Suburban Residential District. The purpose of the R-2 district is to preserve existing residential 
subdivisions throughout the County and provide for compatible infill development in those areas.  
 
Preliminary Comments 
 
Natural Resources Article 8-1809 (j)(2) states that a change to a Critical Area designation may 
be granted by a local approving authority if the proposed Critical Area classification: 
1. Conforms to the State Critical Area mapping criteria; 
2. (a) Is based on land uses or natural features in existence as of June 1, 2002 in the 
Atlantic Coastal Bays; or 
2. (b) For areas included in the Critical Area due to remapping, is based on land uses or 
natural features in existence at the time of the remapping. 
3. Follows the local jurisdiction’s documented mapping methodology for Critical Area 
classifications at the time of original program adoption; and 
Attachment 2
ITEM 12
12 - 18

<!-- page 100 -->
4. Is consistent with the purposes, policies, and goals of this subtitle and all criterial of 
the Commission. 
 
COMAR 27.01.02.03 lists the State Critical Area mapping criteria for IDAs: 
Intensely Development Areas are those areas where residential, commercial, institutional, or 
industrial developed land uses predominate, and where relatively little natural habitat occurs. 
This land classification shall have at least one of the following features: 
1. Housing density is at least four dwelling units per acre;  
2. Industrial, institutional, or commercial uses are concentrated in the area; or  
3. Public sewer and water collection and distribution systems are currently serving the 
area and housing density is greater than three dwelling units per acre. 
 
The County will need to provide additional information detailing how the proposed mapping 
mistake meets the above requirements laid out in the law and the mapping criteria laid out in the 
regulations. Based on the information provided with this application, it does not appear that 
Parcel 268 conforms to the State Critical Area mapping criteria for IDAs, considering the density 
on the property. Based on aerial imagery, it does not appear that the use on the property has 
changed since it was mapped as RCA in 2002.  
 
Thank you for the opportunity to provide preliminary review and comment. Please feel free to 
contact me with any questions: 410.260.3479 or kathryn.hayden@maryland.gov.  
 
Sincerely, 
 
Kathryn Hayden 
Planner

JOB SUMMARY: This individual will review and evaluate applications for new liquor licenses, license 
transfers, requests for changes to an existing license or the licensed premises and identify potential 
compliance issues. The position is responsible for conducting both field work—including on-site 
inspections, meetings with applicants and licensees, compliance checks, and investigations of 
complaints—and office work, including assisting with license renewals, reviewing new applications, 
preparing written reports, coordinate with law enforcement and State agencies and presenting 
findings to the Board of License Commissioners (Board). This position requires approximately 
50% field inspections and investigations and 50% office-based review and reporting. The 
Liquor License Inspector reports directly to the Liquor License Administrator and the Board, under the 
supervision of the Director of Development, Review and Permitting. 
GENERAL REQUIREMENTS: 
•
Pre-employment background check, fingerprinting and Criminal Background Check - State
and Federal
•
Safety sensitive position subject to Drug and Alcohol Testing
•
Possession of a valid driver’s license, and a motor vehicle history with less than 4 points
•
Available to work weekends and evenings when needed
•
Prior law enforcement experience is required
ESSENTIAL JOB DUTIES AND RESPONSIBILITIES: 
Field Work 
•
Conduct investigations and prepare clear, detailed written reports of inspections, compliance
checks, and complaint investigations for review by the Board and/or Liquor License
Administrator.
•
Meet with applicants/ licensees to review plans and premises for new license requests or
modifications/ special temporary changes to a licensed premises. Identify potential problems
and make suggestions that would help to avoid potential violations of the Alcoholic Beverage
Article and the Board’s Rules and Regulations.
•
Inspect special events, check properties for license restriction violations and investigate certain
types of complaints which may occur on weekends and/or evenings.
•
Visit and investigate licensed premises that are suspected of violations or improper practices
that may precipitate a violation, and document findings in a report.

Mr. Theodore J. Elder

Worcester County Commissioners

RE: Request to Increase Hotel Rental Tax (Room Tax) Rate to 6% 
Dear President Elder and Distinguished Worcester County Commissioners: 
On behalf of the Ocean City Mayor and City Council, I respectfully request to be 
placed on the October 16, 2025 County Commissioners meeting agenda to present a 
proposal to increase the Worcester County Hotel Rental Tax (commonly known as the 
room tax) from 5% to 6%. 
During the 2024 session of the Maryland General Assembly, Worcester County, 
along with the other Eastern Shore Code Counties, successfully secured legislation 
authorizing Code Counties to increase the maximum Hotel Rental Tax rate from 5% 
to 6%. Implementing this increase requires a unanimous vote of the County 
Commissioners. 
MAYOR 
Richard W. Meehan 
CITY COUNCIL 
Matthew M. James 
President 
Anthony J. Deluca 
Secretary 
John F. Gehrig, Jr. 
Jacob H. Mitrecic 
Carol Proctor 
Will Savage 
Larry R. Yates 
CITY MANAGER
Terence J. McGean, PE 
CITY CLERK 
Diana L. Chavis, MMC 
Ocean City believes that now is the appropriate time to adopt the increase, primarily due to the 
rising costs of providing essential public safety services throughout Worcester County, and 
especially in Ocean City. Costs for police, fire, and emergency medical services have risen 
dramatically over the past two years. 
Recent changes to Maryland police training requirements eliminated our seasonal police 
program, requiring us to add ten new full-time officers. At the same time, strong statewide 
demand for qualified police officers has increased salaries, which is reflected in our new 
collective bargaining agreement. Over the next three years, police salaries and benefits alone are 
projected to increase by $ 13 million. 
Similarly, due to nationwide shortages ofEMTs and paramedics, our part-time pool has 
decreased from more than 100 pre-pandemic to fewer than 35 today. At the same time, an aging 
year-round population in Ocean City and West Ocean City has driven increased calls for service, 
with 40 occasions in 2022 when no ambulance crews were available. To meet demand, we have 
added 18 full-time EMT/paramedic positions, along with additional equipment purchases, raising 
the department's annual budget by more than $1.5 million.

MEMORANDUM

Candace Savage, CGFM, Deputy Chief Administrative Officer 
FROM: 
Dallas Baker Jr., P.E., Director 
DATE: 
September 11, 2025 
SUBJECT: 
Out-of-County Waste Fines & Suspensions 
Public Works respectfully requests the Commissioners’ approval to implement a penalty 
and suspension schedule targeting commercial haulers who dispose of Out-of-County waste at the 
Central Site Lane Landfill, in violation of County regulations. 
Recently, Wicomico County has encountered operational challenges resulting in their 
landfill reaching capacity faster than projected. In response, Wicomico has instituted a structured 
schedule of escalating fines and suspensions to deter the import of additional waste. 
Since Wicomico adopted this policy, Worcester County has observed a noticeable uptick 
in waste volumes at Central Site Lane. Spot checks have confirmed that a portion of this increase 
is due to Out-of-County waste, which is prohibited under § PW 2-201.d, stating: 
“No solid waste collected outside of Worcester County shall be deposited in any public 
landfill.” 
To preserve the longevity and integrity of our landfill and deter violations, Public Works 
proposes the following enforcement structure for commercial haulers found in violation: 
Proposed Penalty Schedule 
•
1st Offense: $500 fine
•
2nd Offense: $1,000 fine + 1-week suspension
•
3rd Offense: $1,000 fine + 1-month suspension
•
4th Offense: 1-year suspension
Violations would be tracked over a rolling 12-month period. All suspensions would require at 
least 30 days’ notice to the hauler and an opportunity for a hearing before the County 
Commissioners, in accordance with: 
•
§ PW 2-104 – Revocation or Suspension of License
•
§ PW 2-105 – Notice and Hearing Requirements
ITEM 18
18-1

<!-- page 114 -->
2 
For comparison, Wicomico County’s penalty structure is outlined below. Please note, 
Wicomico is a Charter County which State law allows for a higher fine structure than Code 
Counties such as Worcester.  
Wicomico County Penalty Schedule 
•
1st Offense: Two-year probation + $1,000 civil penalty
•
2nd Offense: $2,500 fine
•
3rd Offense: $5,000 fine
•
4th Offense: $5,000 fine + permanent prohibition from landfill use
We welcome your guidance and support in ensuring the continued responsible operation
of Worcester County’s landfill resources. 
Please let me know if there are any questions.

CONTRACT BY AND 
BETWEEN THE 
COUNTY OF 
WORCESTER AND 
CFG HEALTH 
SYSTEMS, L.L.C. 
This Agreement for Incarcerated Individuals Health Care Services (hereinafter, the “Agreement”) 
entered into by and between the COUNTY COMMISSIONERS OF WORCESTER COUNTY, a 
political subdivision in the State of MARYLAND (hereinafter, the “Client”), and CFG HEALTH 
SYSTEMS, L.L.C., a New Jersey limited liability company registered to do business in the State of 
Maryland, and CFG DENTAL CORRECTIONAL CARE, L.L.C., a New Jersey limited liability 
company registered to do business in the State of Maryland (collectively referred to herein as “Company”). 
RECITALS 
WHEREAS, the Client is charged by law with the responsibility for administering, managing, and 
supervising the health care delivery system of the Worcester County Detention Center located at 5022 
Joyner Road, Snow Hill, Maryland 21863 (hereinafter, "Jail"); and 
WHEREAS, the objective of the Client is to provide for the delivery of quality health care to the 
Incarcerated Individuals and Detainees of the Jail (hereinafter, “Jail Population”), in accordance with 
applicable law; and 
WHEREAS, Company is in the business of administering correctional health care services and 
desires to administer such services on behalf of the Client to the Jail Population under the terms and 
conditions hereof. 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the covenants and promises hereinafter made, 
the Parties hereto agree as follows: 
DEFINITIONS 
“As Soon As Possible” – This reference shall mean as soon as the staffing pattern provided for herein, the 
Client’s level of custody staff, and conditions within the Client’s custody facility all taken together permit, 
at all times it being understood that Company cannot provide services without reasonably timely and secure 
access to patients.  
Client Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees – An Incarcerated Individual/Detainee held under the jurisdiction 
of the Client. Client Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees may be housed in the Jail or in another jurisdiction's 
correctional facility. However, Client Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees housed in another jurisdiction are 
not covered by the provisions of this Agreement unless Company administers health care services at the 
other jurisdiction's facility and is specifically set forth below. 
Contract Year – The initial, and any successive, twelve (12) month period beginning with the effective date 
of the Agreement. 
ITEM 19
19-2

<!-- page 117 -->
Covered Persons – An Incarcerated Individual/Detainee of the Jail who is: (1) part of the Jail's MADP; and 
(2) Fit for Confinement; and (3)(a) incarcerated in the Jail; or (b) on work release status while they are at
the facility. NOTE: Covered Persons include Other Client Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees for purposes
of delivery of basic health care services, however, the cost of certain services provided to Other Client
Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees are borne by the Client as set forth in Section 5.0.
Detainee – An adult or juvenile individual whose sentence has not yet been adjudicated and is held as a pre-
trial detainee or other individual held in lawful custody. 
Fit for Confinement – A determination made by Company authorized personnel that an Incarcerated 
Individual/Detainee is medically stable and has been medically cleared for acceptance into the Jail. Such 
determination shall only be made after resolution of any injury or illness requiring immediate transportation 
and treatment at a hospital or similar facility. 
Health Assessment – A health assessment conducted by a qualified nurse or Provider, with the consent of 
a Covered Person, within fourteen (14) days of the Covered Person’s booking into the facility.  
Health Care Staff – Medical, mental health, and support staff provided or managed by Company. 
Hospitalization – Admission of a Covered Person to a licensed health care facility for inpatient treatment. 
Incarcerated Individual – An adult or juvenile individual who is being incarcerated for the term of their 
adjudicated sentence. 
Laws – Any applicable federal, state, or local statutes, codes, ordinances, regulations or court rules and 
orders. 
Licensed Independent Practitioner – An advanced level healthcare professional such as a Nurse Practitioner, 
Physician Assistant, or Clinical Nurse Specialist. 
Medical Record – A record of the care and treatment provided to a Covered Person which is generated or 
housed at the Facility and does not include materials, documents, or records which are subject to an 
evidentiary privilege, designated or considered Patient Safety Work Product, or otherwise deemed 
confidential pursuant to the Federal Patient Safety and Quality Improvement Act of 2005, 42 USC § 299b-
22.  
Monthly Average Daily Population (MADP) – The average number of Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees 
housed in the Jail on a daily basis for the period of one month. The MADP shall include, but separately list, 
Other Client Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees. The MADP shall be figured by summing the daily 
population for the Jails and Other Client Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees (as determined by a count 
performed at the same time each day) for each day of the month and dividing this sum by the total number 
of days in the month. Jail records shall be made available to Company upon request to verify the MADP. 
Persons on home confinement, housed outside of the Jail, and parolees and escapees shall not be considered 
part of the Jail's MADP. 
NCCHC – The National Commission on Correctional Health Care. 
MCCS – Maryland Commission on Correctional Standards 
Other Client Incarcerated Individual/Detainee – An Incarcerated Individual/Detainee under the jurisdiction 
of another Client, state or federal agency, who is being housed in the Jail. 
ITEM 19
19-3

<!-- page 118 -->
Provider - A physician or Licensed Independent Practitioner employed by or contracted with Company 
providing services under this Agreement. 
Receiving Screening - A health screening conducted, with the consent of a Covered Person, after the 
Covered Person is booked into the Correctional Facility by Client personnel.  
Specialty Services – Medical services that require Providers to be trained and/or certified in a specialty such 
as obstetrics, gynecology, or dermatology or other specialized field of medicine, excluding services that are 
otherwise provided for in this Agreement. 
ARTICLE I 
SERVICES 
1.0 
SCOPE OF SERVICES. Company shall administer health care services and related administrative 
services at the Jail according to the terms and provisions as stated in the attached Staffing Matrix 
labeled EXHIBIT A. The costs of the various health care services shall be borne by Company, or 
the Client as set forth in this Article.  This Agreement, including the attachments, and documents 
incorporated herein as the Exhibits constitutes the complete understanding between the parties with 
respect to the terms and conditions set forth herein and supersede all previous written or oral 
agreements and representations. 
1.1 
GENERAL HEALTH CARE SERVICES. Company will arrange and bear the cost of the following 
health care services: 
1.1.1 
RECEIVING SCREENING. A receiving screening of a Covered Person shall be performed 
as soon as possible after the Covered Person’s booking into the Jail, not to exceed 24 hours 
after the Covered Person’s arrival at the Jail. 
1.1.2 
PATIENTS WITH CHRONIC DISEASE AND OTHER SPECIAL NEEDS.  Patients 
identified with chronic disease and/or special needs, and to whom the Company and 
provider have access, shall have a physician and/or midlevel evaluation, in person or via 
tele-medicine, within five (5) business days of their intake, which must include a treatment 
plan. 
1.1.3 
HEALTH ASSESSMENT. Company staff shall complete a health assessment of all 
Covered Persons to whom Company staff has access, as soon as possible, but no later than 
fourteen (14) calendar days after the Incarcerated Individual/Detainee's arrival at the Jail.  
The health assessment shall follow current Maryland Commission on Correctional 
Standards (MCCS) guidelines. 
1.1.4 
SCHEDULED SICK CALL. A qualified healthcare professional shall conduct sick calls 
for Covered Persons on a timely basis and in a clinical setting. 
1.1.4.1 All Incarcerated Individual, regardless of housing assignment, are given 
the opportunity to submit written health care requests at least daily.  
1.1.4.2 Health care requests refer to petitions for medical, dental, or mental health 
services.  
1.1.4.3 A face-to-face encounter for a health care request is conducted by a 
qualified health care professional within 24 hours of receipt by health staff. 
ITEM 19
19-4

<!-- page 119 -->
Face to face may include in person, telemedicine, agency providers and 
PRN staff.  
1.1.4.4 All aspects of the health care request process, from review and 
prioritization to subsequent encounter, are documented, dated, and timed. 
1.1.5 
TELEHEALTH. Health services may be provided via Telehealth, in accordance with the 
Laws of Maryland, when deemed appropriate by Company personnel with managerial 
authority.  
1.1.6 
NON-URGENT CONSULTS. Incarcerated Individual shall be seen within forty-five (45) 
days of referral, or as soon as both an outside provider appointment is available and custody 
staff are able to transport.  
1.1.7 
MEDICATION MANAGEMENT. Company will ensure each new intake, to whom 
Company has access, have resumed their verified medication regime within 24 hours of 
the medication verification.  
1.1.8 
CONTINUING MEDICATION FOR HIV & HCV.  Company will ensure any eligible 
Incarcerated Individual, known to have HIV or HCV who has been on treatment continues 
to receive treatment assuming both the patient and medication are available, and that 
Company has access to the patient. 
1.1.9 
PLACING TSTs (TUBERCULOSIS SKIN TESTS). Company will ensure all eligible 
Incarcerated Individual or resident TSTs are placed, read, and recorded within 72 hours 
after the Incarcerated Individual’s intake or resident’s admission. Company will also place 
TSTs on staff where indicated. 
1.2 
AMBULANCE SERVICE. Company shall be responsible for the provision or cost of any 
ambulance services.  
1.3 
BODY CAVITY SEARCHES/COLLECTION OF PHYSICAL EVIDENCE. Company Health 
Care Staff will not perform body cavity searches, nor collect physical evidence (blood, hair, semen, 
saliva, etc.). 
1.4 
DENTAL - Company shall arrange and bear the cost of dental services, including but not limited 
to exams and treatment (e.g., emergency fillings and extractions) of all Covered Persons and Other 
Client Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees. 
1.5 
ELECTIVE MEDICAL CARE - NOT COVERED. Company shall not be responsible for the 
provision or cost of any elective care. In the event a member of the Jail Population requires elective 
care, the Incarcerated Individual/Detainee or Client shall be responsible for all costs. Elective 
medical care shall be defined as care which, if not provided, would not, in the sole opinion of 
Company's Chief Medical Officer or designee, cause the Incarcerated Individual/Detainee's health 
to deteriorate or cause harm to the Incarcerated Individual/Detainee's wellbeing. Decisions 
concerning elective medical care shall be consistent with the applicable American Medical 
Association (AMA) Standards. 
1.6 
HOSPITALIZATION. Company shall be responsible for the provision or cost of hospitalization 
services, excluding mental health hospitalization. 
ITEM 19
19-5

<!-- page 120 -->
1.7 
LONG TERM CARE. Company shall not be responsible for the provision or cost of any long-term 
care facility services. In the event that a member of the Jail Population requires skilled care, 
custodial care or other services of a long-term care facility, the Client shall bear the cost. 
1.8 
BEHAVIORAL HEALTH CARE. Company shall arrange and bear the cost of on-site mental 
health services for Covered Persons which shall include initial evaluations, medication 
management, individual therapy and follow-up contacts, triaging and managing referrals, crisis 
management, suicide intervention and monitoring, basic community linkage and re-entry, and 
continuity of care. Company shall not be responsible for the provision or cost of any off-site or 
inpatient mental health services. The Client shall be responsible for the provision and cost of off-
site or inpatient mental health services for the Jail Population.  Company may assist with promoting 
the system linkage between the Jail and community inpatient psychiatric services.  Company may 
also assist with any specialized training for facility staff (e.g., suicide prevention, mentally ill 
offenders) upon Client’s request.  Company shall also assist with CQI efforts to assure quality 
services are provided. 
1.9 
PATHOLOGY/RADIOLOGY SERVICES. Company shall be responsible for the provision or cost 
of any professional or technical component pathology or radiology services.  
1.10 
PREGNANT COVERED PERSONS. Company shall arrange and bear the cost of on-site health 
care services for any pregnant Covered Person in accordance with MCCS standards and this 
Agreement, but Company shall not arrange or bear the cost of any health care services for newborns 
or infants. To the extent off-site health care services are required for any pregnant Covered Person, 
Company shall make arrangements for rendering such care. 
1.11 
SPECIALTY SERVICES. Company shall be responsible for the provision or cost of any necessary 
Specialty Services.  
1.12 
VISION CARE. Company shall be responsible for the provision of eyeglasses or any other vision 
services other than care for eye injuries or diseases where medically necessary. 
1.13 
ADDICTION MEDICINE TREATMENT. Company shall be responsible for the provision and 
cost of any medication-assisted addiction treatment services, with the exception of Methadone and 
counseling services, for which the Client shall bear the cost.  
1.14 
OFFICE EQUIPMENT AND SUPPLIES. Company shall be responsible for the provision or cost 
of any and all office equipment and office supplies including, but not limited to, books, medical 
record folders, and forms as required for the administrative operations of the medical unit. All 
office equipment shall remain the Company’s property upon termination of this Agreement.  
1.15 
MEDICAL SUPPLIES/EQUIPMENT. Company shall provide and bear the cost of medical 
supplies (i.e. alcohol prep pads, syringes, etc.) and equipment (i.e. thermometers, scales, etc.) 
required to administer the services required by the terms of the Agreement. Company will create 
an asset tab upon transition into the Jail as soon as reasonably possible. All medical equipment 
currently in Client’s possession will remain in the Jail during Company’s transition, including, but 
not limited to, AED, med cart, and emergency equipment. All medical equipment shall remain the 
property of the Client upon termination of this Agreement. 
1.16 
MEDICAL WASTE. Company shall arrange and bear the cost of removing and properly disposing 
of medical waste material generated while fulfilling its duties under this Agreement in accordance 
with applicable laws/rules. 
ITEM 19
19-6

<!-- page 121 -->
1.17 
DISCHARGE PLANNING.  Company shall ensure all patients receive a discharge planning 
assessment.  
1.18 
PHARMACY SERVICES. Company shall provide monitoring of pharmacy usage as well as a 
Preferred Medication List. Except as provided below, Company shall bear the cost of all 
prescription and non-prescription over-the-counter medications prescribed by a duly licensed 
Company Provider for a Covered Person. 
1.18.1 GENERAL. Prescribing, dispensing, and administering of medication shall comply with 
all State and Federal laws and regulations and all medications shall be dispensed under the 
supervision of a duly authorized, appropriately licensed, or certified Provider. 
1.18.2 EXCEPTIONS. Company shall bear the cost of all prescription medication except 
Methadone. 
ARTICLE II 
HEALTH CARE STAFF 
2.0 
STAFFING HOURS. Company shall provide or arrange for the provision of Health Care Staff 
necessary to render the health care services contemplated in Article I as set forth in the staffing plan 
set forth in EXHIBIT A,  which is attached hereto and incorporated herein and made a part hereof. 
Company reserves the right to assign the staff in EXHIBIT A to shift coverage as necessary based 
on operation needs to provide the health care services under this Agreement. 
2.0.1 
Additional hours may be provided if mutually agreed upon by both Parties in writing. 
2.0.2 
Company shall provide or arrange for the provision of an on-call Provider available by 
telephone 24 hours per day and 7 days per week. 
2.0.3 
Company shall make reasonable efforts to supply the staffing levels contained in this 
section, however, failure to continuously supply all of the required staffing due to labor 
market demands or other factors outside the control of Company, after such reasonable 
efforts have been made, shall not constitute a breach of this Agreement  The Company may 
use tele-health, Agency, PRN staff, and over-time to meet the staffing requirements 
outlined in the Staffing Matrix in Exhibit A. Considering Client’s concerns regarding 
Company staffing with Agency personnel, Company shall obtain written approval from the 
Client before hiring, contracting, or partnering with any Agency personnel wherein Client’s 
approval shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
2.1 
STAFFING LEVELS WAIVER. Based on actual staffing needs as affected by medical 
emergencies, riots, increased or decreased Incarcerated Individual/Detainee population, and other 
unforeseen circumstances, certain increases or decreases in staffing requirements may be waived 
as agreed to by the Client and Company. 
2.2 
STAFF SCREENING. The Client shall timely screen Company’s proposed Health Care Staff, 
employees, agents and/or subcontractors providing services at the Jail to ensure they do not constitute a 
security risk.  The Client shall have final approval of Company’s Health Care Staff, employees, agents 
and/or subcontractors regarding security/background clearance but shall not unreasonably delay or 
withhold such approval. Client represents and warrants that it will provide any such screenings in good 
faith and in accordance with all applicable Laws.

19-7

ARTICLE III 
ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES 
3.0 
MONTHLY REPORTS. Company shall submit monthly health care reports concerning the overall 
operation of the health care services program rendered pursuant to this Agreement and the general 
health of the Jail Population. Any specific data/information requested by the Client in these reports 
shall be conveyed to Company with reasonable notice.  
3.1 
WEEKLY AND/OR DAILY MEETINGS. As requested by the Client, Company shall meet as 
agreed, or as soon thereafter as possible, with the Client, or designee, concerning health care 
services within the Jail and any proposed changes in health-related procedures or other matters, 
which both Parties deem necessary. 
3.2 
MEDICAL RECORDS MANAGEMENT. Company shall provide the following medical records 
management services: 
3.2.1 
MEDICAL RECORDS. Company Health Care Staff shall maintain, cause, or require the 
maintenance of complete and accurate electronic medical records (CorEMR) for Covered 
Persons who have received health care services. Medical records shall be kept separate 
from Covered Person's confinement records. A complete copy of the individual medical 
record shall be available to accompany each Covered Person who is transferred from the 
Jail to another location for off-site services or transferred to another institution. Company 
will keep medical records confidential and shall not release any information contained in 
any medical record except as required by published Jail policies, by a court order or by 
applicable law. Upon termination of this Agreement, all medical records shall be delivered 
to and remain with the Client, as the Client’s property. 
3.2.2 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS. Each medical record shall be maintained in accordance 
with the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act of 1996 ("HIPAA"), and any 
other applicable state or federal privacy statute or regulation. 
3.2.3 
RECORDS AVAILABILITY. As needed to administer the terms of this Agreement, 
Company shall make available to Client, unless otherwise specifically prohibited, at 
Client's request, all records, documents, and other papers relating to the direct delivery of 
health care services to the Jail Population hereunder. Company shall not be required to 
make available records which are privileged under applicable state or federal Laws, with 
the exception that the Client may access patient medical records related to the direct 
delivery of health care services to the Jail Population, but only as permitted by state and 
federal law. 
ARTICLE IV 
PERSONS COVERED UNDER THIS AGREEMENT 
4.0 
GENERAL. Except as otherwise provided in this Agreement, Company shall only be required to 
arrange for health care services under this Agreement to be provided to Covered Persons. 
4.1 EMERGENCY MEDICAL CARE FOR JAIL EMPLOYEES AND VISITORS. Company 
shall arrange for on-site first response emergency medical care as required for jail employees, 
contractors, and visitors to the Jail. The medical treatment shall be limited to the extent 
ITEM 19
19-8

<!-- page 123 -->
reasonably necessary to stabilize and facilitate the individual's referral to a medical facility or 
personal physician.  
4.2 RELEASE FROM CUSTODY. The Client acknowledges and agrees that Company is 
responsible for the payment of costs associated with services rendered to Covered Persons as 
set forth in this Agreement only when such persons remain in the custody of, or under the 
jurisdiction of, the Jail. In no event shall Company be responsible for payment of any costs 
associated with any services rendered to any individual when said individual is released from 
the custody of, or no longer under the jurisdiction of the Jail including, but not limited to, 
releasees, parolees and escapees. Furthermore, in no event shall Company be responsible for 
payment of costs associated with any medical services rendered to a Covered Person when said 
Covered Person is injured outside the Jail facility during transport to or from the Jail. 
ARTICLE V 
PERSONS NOT COVERED OR PARTIALLY COVERED UNDER THIS AGREEMENT 
5.0 
OTHER CLIENT INCARCERATED INDIVIDUALS/DETAINEES. Company shall be responsible 
for arranging and the costs of health assessments, including receiving screening, dental, sick call, over-
the counter medications, medical supplies, and medical waste services for Other Client Incarcerated 
Individuals/Detainees to whom the Company has access, including costs of individuals/detainees 
subject to federal jurisdiction (e.g. U.S. Marshal individuals/detainees). The cost of all prescription 
medication and all other health care expenses shall be paid by the agency responsible for the Other 
Client Incarcerated Individual/Detainee, including those services listed in Article I of this Agreement 
not otherwise specifically covered in this Section 5.0, and all other medically related expenses 
associated with Other Client Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees. 
5.1 
CLIENT 
INCARCERATED 
INDIVIDUALS/DETAINEES 
HOUSED 
IN 
OTHER 
JURISDICTIONS OR OUTSIDE THE JAIL. Company shall not be responsible for arranging the 
medical care or treatment for Client Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees housed in other counties or 
jurisdictions. The Client or other agency with legal responsibility for the medical care of such 
persons shall be responsible for all medical expenses associated with the care and treatment of 
Client Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees removed from the Jail, including, but not limited to the 
services listed in Article I of this Agreement and any other health care related expenses associated 
with said Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees, unless the Incarcerated Individual/Detainee is housed 
in a facility where Company provides Incarcerated Individual/Detainee health care services. 
Company shall not be responsible for arranging the medical care or treatment for Client 
Incarcerated Individuals/Detainees housed outside the Jail. 
5.2 
INJURIES PRIOR TO INCARCERATION, FIT FOR CONFINEMENT, AND ESCAPED 
INCARCERATED INDIVIDUAL/DETAINEES.  Company shall not be responsible for the cost 
of providing off-site medical care for injuries incurred by an arrested person prior to incarceration 
at the Jail, including, but not limited to, medical services provided to any arrested person prior to 
the person’s booking and confinement in the Jail. In addition, Company shall not be responsible 
for the cost of any medical treatment or health care services necessary to medically stabilize any 
arrested person presented at intake by an arresting agency with a life-threatening injury or illness 
or in immediate need of emergency medical care. Company shall provide such care as is medically 
necessary until the arrested person can be transported to a medical care facility by the arresting 
agency or their designee. The arresting authority or the Client shall bear the cost of, and be 
responsible for, all reasonable and necessary medical services or health care services of the 
individual until such time as the arresting authority can present a medically stable individual that 
ITEM 19
19-9

<!-- page 124 -->
is Fit for Confinement. To the extent Company is billed for medical services provided to an 
individual who is not Fit for Confinement the Client shall reimburse Company for all such costs. 
Company shall not charge an additional fee simply to examine an individual to determine if he is 
suitably Fit for Confinement. 
ARTICLE VI 
COST OF SERVICES NOT COVERED UNDER THIS AGREEMENT 
6.0 
SERVICES NOT LISTED. If the Client requests Company provide any health care services that
